Input

{
  "filePath": "/source/standards/karpathy-guidelines.mdc"
}

Output

<path>/source/standards/karpathy-guidelines.mdc</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Use when writing, reviewing, or refactoring code to avoid overcomplication, make surgical changes, surface assumptions, and define verifiable success criteria.
3: alwaysApply: true
4: ---
5: 
6: # Karpathy behavioral guidelines
7: 
8: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Merge with project-specific instructions as needed.
9: 
10: **Tradeoff:** These guidelines bias toward caution over speed. For trivial tasks, use judgment.
11: 
12: ## 1. Think Before Coding
13: 
14: **Don't assume. Don't hide confusion. Surface tradeoffs.**
15: 
16: Before implementing:
17: 
18: - State your assumptions explicitly. If uncertain, ask.
19: - If multiple interpretations exist, present them - don't pick silently.
20: - If a simpler approach exists, say so. Push back when warranted.
21: - If something is unclear, stop. Name what's confusing. Ask.
22: 
23: ## 2. Simplicity First
24: 
25: **Minimum code that solves the problem. Nothing speculative.**
26: 
27: - No features beyond what was asked.
28: - No abstractions for single-use code.
29: - No "flexibility" or "configurability" that wasn't requested.
30: - No error handling for impossible scenarios.
31: - If you write 200 lines and it could be 50, rewrite it.
32: 
33: Ask yourself: "Would a senior engineer say this is overcomplicated?" If yes, simplify.
34: 
35: ## 3. Surgical Changes
36: 
37: **Touch only what you must. Clean up only your own mess.**
38: 
39: When editing existing code:
40: 
41: - Don't "improve" adjacent code, comments, or formatting.
42: - Don't refactor things that aren't broken.
43: - Match existing style, even if you'd do it differently.
44: - If you notice unrelated dead code, mention it - don't delete it.
45: 
46: When your changes create orphans:
47: 
48: - Remove imports/variables/functions that YOUR changes made unused.
49: - Don't remove pre-existing dead code unless asked.
50: 
51: The test: Every changed line should trace directly to the user's request.
52: 
53: ## 4. Goal-Driven Execution
54: 
55: **Define success criteria. Loop until verified.**
56: 
57: Transform tasks into verifiable goals:
58: 
59: - "Add validation" → "Write tests for invalid inputs, then make them pass"
60: - "Fix the bug" → "Write a test that reproduces it, then make it pass"
61: - "Refactor X" → "Ensure tests pass before and after"
62: 
63: For multi-step tasks, state a brief plan:
64: 
65: ```
66: 1. [Step] → verify: [check]
67: 2. [Step] → verify: [check]
68: 3. [Step] → verify: [check]
69: ```
70: 
71: Strong success criteria let you loop independently. Weak criteria ("make it work") require constant clarification.
72: 
73: ---
74: 
75: **These guidelines are working if:** fewer unnecessary changes in diffs, fewer rewrites due to overcomplication, and clarifying questions come before implementation rather than after mistakes.

(End of file - total 75 lines)
</content>

read · completed

Input

{
  "filePath": "/source/standards/ponytail.mdc"
}

Output

<path>/source/standards/ponytail.mdc</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode. Always pick the simplest solution that works.
3: globs:
4: alwaysApply: true
5: ---
6: 
7: # Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode
8: 
9: You are a lazy senior developer. Lazy means efficient, not careless. The best code is the code never written.
10: 
11: Before writing any code, stop at the first rung that holds:
12: 
13: 1. Does this need to be built at all? (YAGNI)
14: 2. Does it already exist in this codebase? Reuse the helper, util, or pattern that's already here, don't re-write it.
15: 3. Does the standard library already do this? Use it.
16: 4. Does a native platform feature cover it? Use it.
17: 5. Does an already-installed dependency solve it? Use it.
18: 6. Can this be one line? Make it one line.
19: 7. Only then: write the minimum code that works.
20: 
21: The ladder runs after you understand the problem, not instead of it: read the task and the code it touches, trace the real flow end to end, then climb.
22: 
23: Bug fix = root cause, not symptom: a report names a symptom. Grep every caller of the function you touch and fix the shared function once — one guard there is a smaller diff than one per caller, and patching only the path the ticket names leaves a sibling caller still broken.
24: 
25: Rules:
26: 
27: - No abstractions that weren't explicitly requested.
28: - No new dependency if it can be avoided.
29: - No boilerplate nobody asked for.
30: - Deletion over addition. Boring over clever. Fewest files possible.
31: - Shortest working diff wins, but only once you understand the problem. The smallest change in the wrong place isn't lazy, it's a second bug.
32: - Question complex requests: "Do you actually need X, or does Y cover it?"
33: - Pick the edge-case-correct option when two stdlib approaches are the same size, lazy means less code, not the flimsier algorithm.
34: - Mark deliberate simplifications that cut a real corner with a known ceiling (global lock, O(n²) scan, naive heuristic) with a `ponytail:` comment naming the ceiling and upgrade path.
35: 
36: Not lazy about: understanding the problem (read it fully and trace the real flow before picking a rung, a small diff you don't understand is just laziness dressed up as efficiency), input validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs (the platform is never the spec ideal, a clock drifts, a sensor reads off), anything explicitly requested. Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures). Trivial one-liners need no test.

(End of file - total 36 lines)
</content>

Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures).         "text": "---\ndescription: Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode. Always pick the simplest solution that works.\nglobs:\nalwaysApply: true\n---\n\n# Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode\n\nYou are a lazy senior developer. Lazy means efficient, not careless. The best code is the code never written.\n\nBefore writing any code, stop at the first rung that holds:\n\n1. Does this need to be built at all? (YAGNI)\n2. Does it already exist in this codebase? Reuse the helper, util, or pattern that's already here, don't re-write it.\n3. Does the standard library already do this? Use it.\n4. Does a native platform feature cover it? Use it.\n5. Does an already-installed dependency solve it? Use it.\n6. Can this be one line? Make it one line.\n7. Only then: write the minimum code that works.\n\nThe ladder runs after you understand the problem, not instead of it: read the task and the code it touches, trace the real flow end to end, then climb.\n\nBug fix = root cause, not symptom: a report names a symptom. Grep every caller of the function you touch and fix the shared function once — one guard there is a smaller diff than one per caller, and patching only the path the ticket names leaves a sibling caller still broken.\n\nRules:\n\n- No abstractions that weren't explicitly requested.\n- No new dependency if it can be avoided.\n- No boilerplate nobody asked for.\n- Deletion over addition. Boring over clever. Fewest files possible.\n- Shortest working diff wins, but only once you understand the problem. The smallest change in the wrong place isn't lazy, it's a second bug.\n- Question complex requests: \"Do you actually need X, or does Y cover it?\"\n- Pick the edge-case-correct option when two stdlib approaches are the same size, lazy means less code, not the flimsier algorithm.\n- Mark deliberate simplifications that cut a real corner with a known ceiling (global lock, O(n²) scan, naive heuristic) with a `ponytail:` comment naming the ceiling and upgrade path.\n\nNot lazy about: understanding the problem (read it fully and trace the real flow before picking a rung, a small diff you don't understand is just laziness dressed up as efficiency), input validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs (the platform is never the spec ideal, a clock drifts, a sensor reads off), anything explicitly requested. Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures). Trivial one-liners need no test.",

539: +// mgmtNetLinkNetworks returns the management networks referenced by mgmt-net links.
540: +func (c *CLab) mgmtNetLinkNetworks() []*clabtypes.MgmtNet {
541: +	if c.Config.Topology == nil {
542: +		return nil
543: +	}
544: +	var networks []*clabtypes.MgmtNet
545: +	for _, link := range c.Config.Topology.Links {
546: +		if link == nil || link.Link == nil {
547: +			continue
548: +		}
549: +		mgmtNetLink, ok := link.Link.(*clablinks.LinkMgmtNetRaw)
550: +		if !ok {
551: +			continue
552: +		}
553: +		if m, err := c.mgmtNetByName(mgmtNetLink.Network); err == nil {
554: +			networks = append(networks, m)
555: +		}
556: +	}
557: +	return networks
558: +}
559: +
560: +// resolveMgmtNetLinkNetworks populates the bridge names of the non-default management
561: +// networks that mgmt-net links reference without an explicit bridge. The runtime names
562: +// such a bridge when it creates the network, so the bridge of an existing network is
563: +// looked up from the runtime. The lookup neither creates networks nor changes host
564: +// state, so it is safe for dry runs. Networks that do not exist yet are left unresolved.
565: +func (c *CLab) resolveMgmtNetLinkNetworks(ctx context.Context) error {
566: +	var resolved clabtypes.MgmtNetworks
567: +	for _, m := range c.mgmtNetLinkNetworks() {
568: +		if m == c.Config.Mgmt || m.Bridge != "" || slices.Contains(resolved, m) {
569: +			continue
570: +		}
571: +		resolved = append(resolved, m)
572: +
573: +		rt, err := c.mgmtRuntime(c.globalRuntimeName, m)
574: +		if err != nil {
575: +			// runtimes without multiple network support never created extra networks
576: +			log.Debug("Skipping management network resolution", "network", m.Network, "error", err)
577: +			continue
578: +		}
579: +		resolver, ok := rt.(clabruntime.MgmtBridgeResolver)
580: +		if !ok {
581: +			continue
582: +		}
583: +		if err := resolver.ResolveMgmtBridge(ctx); err != nil {
584: +			return err
585: +		}
586: +	}
587: +	return nil
588: +}

592: +func (c *CLab) setMgmtNetLinkBridges() error {
593: +	if err := clablinks.SetMgmtNetUnderlyingBridge(c.Config.Mgmt.Bridge); err != nil {
594: +		return err
595: +	}

598: +			continue
599: +		}
600: +		if err := clablinks.SetMgmtNetworkUnderlyingBridge(m.Network, m.Bridge); err != nil {
601: +			return err
602: +		}
603: +	}
604: +	return nil
605: +}

1037: +// attached to a bridge other than the one the endpoint resolves to, e.g. after a
1038: +// mgmt-net link moved to another management network. mgmt-net endpoints whose

1040: +func bridgeMasterMismatch(ep clablinks.Endpoint, live clablinks.OwnedInterface) bool {
1041: +	node := ep.GetNode()
1042: +	if node.GetLinkEndpointType() != clablinks.LinkEndpointTypeBridge {
1043: +		return false
1044: +	}

1048: +		return false
1049: +	}
1050: +

1052: +}

1064:  The following also should be taken into consideration:
1065:  
1066:  - Nodes using `network-mode` ignore the `mgmt-net` key.

1068:  - [`skip-when-unused`](#skipping-the-management-network) is evaluated per network: a network with the flag set is not created when every node attached to it runs with `network-mode: none`.

1070:  - When a management network is dropped from the `mgmt` list, the next `destroy` removes it together with the networks the topology still defines.

1072:  ### Drivers
1073:  
1074:  The driver specifies the type of network that is created. Either `bridge` or `macvlan`. 
1075: @@ -727,10 +728,37 @@ topology:
1076:  
1077:  In the above example the node `n1` connects with its `e1-1` interface to the management network. This is done by specifying the endpoint with a reserved name `mgmt-net` and defining the name of the interface that should be used in that bridge (`n1-e1-1`).
1078:  
1079:  By specifying `mgmt-net` name of the node in the endpoint definition we tell containerlab to find out which bridge is used by the management network of our lab and use this bridge as the attachment point for our veth pair.
1080:  
1081: +With [multiple management networks](#multiple-management-networks) the brief form attaches to the first network in the `mgmt` list. To attach to a different network, use the [extended `mgmt-net` link](topo-def-file.md#mgmt-net) definition and set the `network` key to the name of the management network:
1082: +
1083: +```yaml
1084: +name: multi
1085: +mgmt:
1086: +  - network: oob
1087: +    ipv4-subnet: 172.31.10.0/24
1088: +  - network: lab
1089: +    ipv4-subnet: 172.31.20.0/24
1090: +    bridge: br-lab
1091: +topology:
1092: +  nodes:
1093: +    r1:
1094: +      kind: linux
1095: +      image: alpine
1096: +      mgmt-net: oob
1097: +  links:
1098: +    - type: mgmt-net
1099: +      network: lab
1100: +      endpoint:
1101: +        node: r1
1102: +        interface: eth1
1103: +      host-interface: r1-eth1
1104: +```
1105: +
1106: +The referenced network must be defined in the `mgmt` list and use the `bridge` driver.
1107: +
1108:  This is best illustrated with the following diagram:
1109:  
1110:  <div class="mxgraph" style="max-width:100%;border:1px solid transparent;margin:0 auto; display:block;" data-mxgraph="{&quot;page&quot;:14,&quot;zoom&quot;:1.5,&quot;highlight&quot;:&quot;#0000ff&quot;,&quot;nav&quot;:true,&quot;check-visible-state&quot;:true,&quot;resize&quot;:true,&quot;url&quot;:&quot;https://raw.githubusercontent.com/srl-labs/containerlab/diagrams/containerlab.drawio&quot;}"></div>
1111:  
1112:  ### MACVLAN links
1113: diff --git a/docs/manual/topo-def-file.md b/docs/manual/topo-def-file.md
1114: index 78ed408..11d44b5 100644
1115: --- a/docs/manual/topo-def-file.md
1116: +++ b/docs/manual/topo-def-file.md
1117: @@ -311,17 +311,20 @@ The mgmt-net link type represents a veth pair that is connected to a container n
1118:      endpoint:
1119:        node: <NodeA-Name>                    # mandatory
1120:        interface: <NodeA-Interface-Name>     # mandatory
1121:        mac: <NodeA-Interface-Mac>            # optional
1122:      host-interface: <interface-name>        # mandatory
1123: +    network: <mgmt-network-name>            # optional
1124:      mtu: <link-mtu>                         # optional
1125:      vars: <link-variables>                  # optional (used in templating)
1126:      labels: <link-labels>                   # optional (used in templating)
1127:  ```
1128:  
1129:  The `host-interface` is the desired interface name that will be attached to the management network in the host namespace.
1130:  
1131: +The `network` selects one of the [multiple management networks](network.md#multiple-management-networks) by its name. When omitted, the link attaches to the first management network. The selected network must use the `bridge` driver.
1132: +
1133:  ###### macvlan
1134:  
1135:  The macvlan link type creates a MACVlan interface with the `host-interface` as its parent interface. The MACVlan interface is then moved to a node's network namespace and renamed to the `endpoint.interface` name.
1136:

168: 	_mgmtBrLinkNodesMu.Lock()
169: 	defer _mgmtBrLinkNodesMu.Unlock()
170: 
171: 	if n, ok := _mgmtBrLinkNodes[network]; ok {
172: 		return n
173: 	}
174: 
175: 	currns, err := ns.GetCurrentNS()
176: 	if err != nil {
177: 		log.Errorf("failed to get current network namespace: %v", err)
178: 		return nil
179: 	}
180: 	n := &mgmtBridgeLinkNode{
181: 		GenericLinkNode: GenericLinkNode{
182: 			shortname: mgmtNetOwnerName,
183: 			endpoints: []Endpoint{},
184: 			nspath:    currns.Path(),
185: 		},
186: 	}
187: 	_mgmtBrLinkNodes[network] = n
188: 
189: 	return n
190: }
191: 
192: // GetMgmtBrLinkNode returns the mgmt-net link node of the default management network.
193: func GetMgmtBrLinkNode() Node { // skipcq: RVV-B0001
194: 	return getMgmtBrLinkNode("")
195: }
196: 
197: // GetMgmtBrLinkNodes returns the mgmt-net link nodes of all management networks
198: // that have been referenced so far, starting with the default network.
199: func GetMgmtBrLinkNodes() []Node {
200: 	var nodes []Node
201: 	if n := getMgmtBrLinkNode(""); n != nil {
202: 		nodes = append(nodes, n)
203: 	}
204: 
205: 	_mgmtBrLinkNodesMu.Lock()
206: 	defer _mgmtBrLinkNodesMu.Unlock()
207: 
208: 	networks := make([]string, 0, len(_mgmtBrLinkNodes))
209: 	for network := range _mgmtBrLinkNodes {
210: 		if network != "" {
211: 			networks = append(networks, network)
212: 		}
213: 	}
214: 	slices.Sort(networks)
215: 
216: 	for _, network := range networks {
217: 		nodes = append(nodes, _mgmtBrLinkNodes[network])
218: 	}
219: 
220: 	return nodes
221: }
222: 
223: // SetMgmtNetUnderlyingBridge sets the bridge of the default management network.
224: func SetMgmtNetUnderlyingBridge(bridge string) error {
225: 	return SetMgmtNetworkUnderlyingBridge("", bridge)
226: }
227: 
228: // SetMgmtNetworkUnderlyingBridge sets the bridge the mgmt-net links of the given
229: // management network attach to. The empty network name selects the default network.
230: func SetMgmtNetworkUnderlyingBridge(network, bridge string) error {
231: 	n := getMgmtBrLinkNode(network)
232: 	if n == nil {
233: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to get the mgmt-net link node for network %q", network)
234: 	}
235: 	n.GenericLinkNode.shortname = bridge
236: 	return nil
237: }

1: package core
2: 
3: import (
4: 	"context"
5: 	"fmt"
6: 	"net/netip"
7: 	"slices"
8: 
9: 	"github.com/charmbracelet/log"
10: 	clabconstants "github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/constants"
11: 	clablinks "github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/links"
12: 	"github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/mgmt"
13: 	clabruntime "github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/runtime"
14: 	clabtypes "github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/types"

17: func (c *CLab) CreateNetwork(ctx context.Context) error {
18: 	defined := c.allMgmtNetworks()
19: 	networks := make(clabtypes.MgmtNetworks, 0, len(defined))
20: 	for _, m := range defined {
21: 		if c.skipMgmtNetwork(m) {
22: 			log.Debug("Skipping management network, no node uses it", "network", m.Network)
23: 			continue
24: 		}
25: 		networks = append(networks, m)
26: 	}
27: 	return c.createNetworks(ctx, networks)
28: }
29: 
30: // createNetworks creates or inspects the given management networks
31: // and labels the nodes with their management bridge.
32: func (c *CLab) createNetworks(ctx context.Context, networks clabtypes.MgmtNetworks) error {
33: 	for _, m := range networks {
34: 		var opts []clabruntime.NetworkCreateOptions
35: 		if addresses := c.staticManagementAddresses(m); len(addresses) != 0 {
36: 			opts = append(opts, clabruntime.NetworkCreateOptions{StaticAddresses: addresses})
37: 		}
38: 
39: 		rt, err := c.mgmtRuntime(c.globalRuntimeName, m)
40: 		if err != nil {
41: 			return err
42: 		}
43: 		// create docker network or use existing one
44: 		if err := rt.CreateNet(ctx, opts...); err != nil {
45: 			return err
46: 		}
47: 
48: 		// VM kinds read the subnets at init, before the runtime resolved them
49: 		for _, cfg := range c.mgmtNetNodes(m) {
50: 			for key, subnet := range map[string]string{
51: 				"DOCKER_NET_V4_ADDR": m.IPv4Subnet,
52: 				"DOCKER_NET_V6_ADDR": m.IPv6Subnet,
53: 			} {
54: 				if value, ok := cfg.Env[key]; ok && value == "" {
55: 					cfg.Env[key] = subnet
56: 				}
57: 			}
58: 		}
59: 	}
60: 
61: 	// save mgmt bridge name as a label
62: 	for _, n := range c.Nodes {
63: 		bridge := c.globalRuntime().Mgmt().Bridge
64: 		if m := c.mgmtNetByNetwork(n.Config().MgmtNet); m != c.Config.Mgmt {
65: 			bridge = m.Bridge
66: 		}
67: 		n.Config().Labels[clabconstants.NodeMgmtNetBr] = bridge
68: 	}
69: 
70: 	return nil
71: }
72: 
73: // mgmtNetNodes returns the configs of the nodes attached to the given management network.
74: func (c *CLab) mgmtNetNodes(m *clabtypes.MgmtNet) []*clabtypes.NodeConfig {
75: 	var configs []*clabtypes.NodeConfig
76: 	for _, node := range c.Nodes {
77: 		if c.mgmtNetByNetwork(node.Config().MgmtNet) == m {
78: 			configs = append(configs, node.Config())
79: 		}
80: 	}
81: 	return configs
82: }
83: 
84: // SyncMgmtHostRoutes reconciles host routes to current macvlan management endpoints.
85: func (c *CLab) SyncMgmtHostRoutes(ctx context.Context) error {
86: 	if c.Config == nil || c.Config.Mgmt == nil {
87: 		return nil
88: 	}
89: 	c.mgmtRouteMu.Lock()
90: 	defer c.mgmtRouteMu.Unlock()
91: 	for _, m := range c.allMgmtNetworks() {
92: 		if m.Driver != clabtypes.MgmtDriverMacvlan || !m.MacvlanAuxEnabled() {
93: 			continue
94: 		}
95: 		rt, err := c.mgmtRuntime(c.globalRuntimeName, m)
96: 		if err != nil {
97: 			return err
98: 		}
99: 		if err := rt.SyncMgmtHostRoutes(ctx); err != nil {
100: 			return err
101: 		}
102: 	}
103: 	return nil
104: }
105: 
106: func (c *CLab) staticManagementAddresses(m *clabtypes.MgmtNet) []netip.Addr {
107: 	var addresses []netip.Addr
108: 	for _, config := range c.mgmtNetNodes(m) {
109: 		if address, err := netip.ParseAddr(config.MgmtIPv4Address); err == nil {
110: 			addresses = append(addresses, address)
111: 		}
112: 		if address, err := netip.ParseAddr(config.MgmtIPv6Address); err == nil {
113: 			addresses = append(addresses, address)
114: 		}
115: 	}
116: 	return addresses
117: }
118: 
119: // validateManagementLinks checks that every mgmt-net link references a defined
120: // management network that is backed by a bridge.
121: func (c *CLab) validateManagementLinks() error {
122: 	if c.Config.Topology == nil {
123: 		return nil
124: 	}
125: 	for _, link := range c.Config.Topology.Links {
126: 		if link == nil || link.Link == nil {
127: 			continue
128: 		}
129: 		mgmtNetLink, ok := link.Link.(*clablinks.LinkMgmtNetRaw)
130: 		if !ok {
131: 			continue
132: 		}
133: 		m, err := c.mgmtNetByName(mgmtNetLink.Network)
134: 		if err != nil {
135: 			return fmt.Errorf("mgmt-net link: %w", err)
136: 		}
137: 		if m.Driver == clabtypes.MgmtDriverMacvlan {
138: 			if mgmtNetLink.Network == "" {
139: 				return fmt.Errorf(
140: 					"mgmt-net links require a bridge management network and cannot be used with mgmt.driver %q",
141: 					m.Driver,
142: 				)
143: 			}
144: 			return fmt.Errorf(
145: 				"mgmt-net links require a bridge management network and cannot use management network %q with driver %q",
146: 				m.Network,
147: 				m.Driver,
148: 			)
149: 		}
150: 	}
151: 	return nil
152: }
153: 
154: // mgmtNetLinkNetworks returns the management networks referenced by mgmt-net links.
155: func (c *CLab) mgmtNetLinkNetworks() []*clabtypes.MgmtNet {
156: 	if c.Config.Topology == nil {
157: 		return nil
158: 	}
159: 	var networks []*clabtypes.MgmtNet
160: 	for _, link := range c.Config.Topology.Links {
161: 		if link == nil || link.Link == nil {
162: 			continue
163: 		}
164: 		mgmtNetLink, ok := link.Link.(*clablinks.LinkMgmtNetRaw)
165: 		if !ok {
166: 			continue
167: 		}
168: 		if m, err := c.mgmtNetByName(mgmtNetLink.Network); err == nil {
169: 			networks = append(networks, m)
170: 		}
171: 	}
172: 	return networks
173: }
174: 
175: // resolveMgmtNetLinkNetworks populates the bridge names of the non-default management
176: // networks that mgmt-net links reference without an explicit bridge. The runtime names
177: // such a bridge when it creates the network, so the bridge of an existing network is
178: // looked up from the runtime. The lookup neither creates networks nor changes host
179: // state, so it is safe for dry runs. Networks that do not exist yet are left unresolved.
180: func (c *CLab) resolveMgmtNetLinkNetworks(ctx context.Context) error {
181: 	var resolved clabtypes.MgmtNetworks
182: 	for _, m := range c.mgmtNetLinkNetworks() {
183: 		if m == c.Config.Mgmt || m.Bridge != "" || slices.Contains(resolved, m) {
184: 			continue
185: 		}
186: 		resolved = append(resolved, m)
187: 
188: 		rt, err := c.mgmtRuntime(c.globalRuntimeName, m)
189: 		if err != nil {
190: 			// runtimes without multiple network support never created extra networks
191: 			log.Debug("Skipping management network resolution", "network", m.Network, "error", err)
192: 			continue
193: 		}
194: 		resolver, ok := rt.(clabruntime.MgmtBridgeResolver)
195: 		if !ok {
196: 			continue
197: 		}
198: 		if err := resolver.ResolveMgmtBridge(ctx); err != nil {
199: 			return err
200: 		}
201: 	}
202: 	return nil
203: }
204: 
205: // setMgmtNetLinkBridges points the mgmt-net link nodes of the management networks
206: // that mgmt-net links select at the bridge of that network.
207: func (c *CLab) setMgmtNetLinkBridges() error {
208: 	if err := clablinks.SetMgmtNetUnderlyingBridge(c.Config.Mgmt.Bridge); err != nil {
209: 		return err
210: 	}
211: 	for _, m := range c.mgmtNetLinkNetworks() {
212: 		if m == c.Config.Mgmt || m.Network == "" {
213: 			continue
214: 		}
215: 		if err := clablinks.SetMgmtNetworkUnderlyingBridge(m.Network, m.Bridge); err != nil {
216: 			return err
217: 		}
218: 	}
219: 	return nil
220: }
221: 
222: // AllocateToolManagementIPs assigns addresses when the topology uses containerlab IPAM.
223: func (c *CLab) AllocateToolManagementIPs(ctx context.Context, cfg *clabtypes.NodeConfig) error {
224: 	m := c.mgmtNetByNetwork(cfg.MgmtNet)
225: 	if m.IPAM.Provider == clabtypes.IPAMProviderRuntime {
226: 		return nil
227: 	}
228: 	// tools attach to a network on demand, so it is created even when it is
229: 	// flagged as skipped-when-unused by the topology
230: 	if err := c.createNetworks(ctx, clabtypes.MgmtNetworks{m}); err != nil {
231: 		return err
232: 	}
233: 	reserved, err := c.collectReservedManagementAddresses(ctx, m, nil)
234: 	if err != nil {
235: 		return err
236: 	}
237: 	return mgmt.AllocateManagementIPs(
238: 		ctx,
239: 		m,
240: 		[]*clabtypes.NodeConfig{cfg},
241: 		clabtypes.AllocationOptions{Reserved: reserved},
242: 	)
243: }
244: 
245: // skipMgmtNetwork reports whether the management network m must not be created
246: // because it is not used: skip-when-unused is set on it and no node attached to
247: // it resolves to a network mode that attaches to the management network.
248: func (c *CLab) skipMgmtNetwork(m *clabtypes.MgmtNet) bool {
249: 	if m == nil || !m.SkipWhenUnused {
250: 		return false
251: 	}
252: 
253: 	topo := c.Config.Topology
254: 	if topo == nil || len(topo.Nodes) == 0 {
255: 		return false
256: 	}
257: 
258: 	// a network that mgmt-net links attach to is in use
259: 	if slices.Contains(c.mgmtNetLinkNetworks(), m) {
260: 		return false
261: 	}
262: 
263: 	for name := range topo.Nodes {
264: 		if topo.GetNodeNetworkMode(name) == "none" {
265: 			continue
266: 		}
267: 		if c.mgmtNetByNetwork(topo.GetNodeMgmtNet(name)) == m {
268: 			return false
269: 		}
270: 	}
271: 
272: 	return true
273: }
274: 
275: // mgmtNetworksSkipped reports whether every defined management network is
276: // skipped because it is unused.
277: func (c *CLab) mgmtNetworksSkipped() bool {
278: 	for _, m := range c.allMgmtNetworks() {
279: 		if !c.skipMgmtNetwork(m) {
280: 			return false
281: 		}
282: 	}
283: 	return true
284: }

err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn err\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\treturn nil\n}\n\nfunc (c *CLab) staticManagementAddresses(m *clabtypes.MgmtNet) []netip.Addr {\n\tvar addresses []netip.Addr\n\tfor _, config := range c.mgmtNetNodes(m) {\n\t\tif address, err := netip.ParseAddr(config.MgmtIPv4Address); err == nil {\n\t\t\taddresses = append(addresses, address)\n\t\t}\n\t\tif address, err := netip.ParseAddr(config.MgmtIPv6Address); err == nil {\n\t\t\taddresses = append(addresses, address)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\treturn addresses\n}\n\n// validateManagementLinks checks that every mgmt-net link references a defined\n// management network that is backed by a bridge.\nfunc (c *CLab) validateManagementLinks() error {\n\tif c.Config.Topology == nil {\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\tfor _, link := range c.Config.Topology.Links {\n\t\tif link == nil || link.Link == nil {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tmgmtNetLink, ok := link.Link.(*clablinks.LinkMgmtNetRaw)\n\t\tif !ok {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tm, err := c.mgmtNetByName(mgmtNetLink.Network)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"mgmt-net link: %w\", err)\n\t\t}\n\t\tif m.Driver == clabtypes.MgmtDriverMacvlan {\n\t\t\tif mgmtNetLink.Network == \"\" {\n\t\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\n\t\t\t\t\t\"mgmt-net links require a bridge management network and cannot be used with mgmt.driver %q\",\n\t\t\t\t\tm.Driver,\n\t\t\t\t)\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\n\t\t\t\t\"mgmt-net links require a bridge management network and cannot use management network %q with driver %q\",\n\t\t\t\tm.Network,\n\t\t\t\tm.Driver,\n\t\t\t)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\treturn nil\n}\n\n// mgmtNetLinkNetworks returns the management networks referenced by mgmt-net links.\nfunc (c *CLab) mgmtNetLinkNetworks() []*clabtypes.MgmtNet {\n\tif c.Config.Topology == nil {\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\tvar networks []*clabtypes.MgmtNet\n\tfor _, link := range c.Config.Topology.Links {\n\t\tif link == nil || link.Link == nil {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tmgmtNetLink, ok := link.Link.(*clablinks.LinkMgmtNetRaw)\n\t\tif !ok {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tif m, err := c.mgmtNetByName(mgmtNetLink.Network); err == nil {\n\t\t\tnetworks = append(networks, m)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\treturn networks\n}\n\n// resolveMgmtNetLinkNetworks populates the bridge names of the non-default management\n// networks that mgmt-net links reference without an explicit bridge. The runtime names\n// such a bridge when it creates the network, so the bridge of an existing network is\n// looked up from the runtime. The lookup neither creates networks nor changes host\n// state, so it is safe for dry runs. Networks that do not exist yet are left unresolved.\nfunc (c *CLab) resolveMgmtNetLinkNetworks(ctx context.Context) error {\n\tvar resolved clabtypes.MgmtNetworks\n\tfor _, m := range c.mgmtNetLinkNetworks() {\n\t\tif m == c.Config.Mgmt || m.Bridge != \"\" || slices.Contains(resolved, m) {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tresolved = append(resolved, m)\n\n\t\trt, err := c.mgmtRuntime(c.globalRuntimeName, m)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\t// runtimes without multiple network support never created extra networks\n\t\t\tlog.Debug(\"Skipping management network resolution\", \"network\", m.Network, \"error\", err)\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tresolver, ok := rt.(clabruntime.MgmtBridgeResolver)\n\t\tif !ok {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tif err := resolver.ResolveMgmtBridge(ctx); err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn err\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\treturn nil\n}\n\n// setMgmtNetLinkBridges points the mgmt-net link nodes of the management networks\n// that mgmt-net links select at the bridge of that network.\nfunc (c *CLab) setMgmtNetLinkBridges() error {\n\tif err := clablinks.SetMgmtNetUnderlyingBridge(c.Config.Mgmt.Bridge); err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\tfor _, m := range c.mgmtNetLinkNetworks() {\n\t\tif m == c.Config.Mgmt || m.Network == \"\" {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tif err := clablinks.SetMgmtNetworkUnderlyingBridge(m.Network, m.Bridge); err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn err\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\treturn nil\n}\n\n// AllocateToolManagementIPs assigns addresses when the topology uses containerlab IPAM.\nfunc (c *CLab) AllocateToolManagementIPs(ctx context.Context, cfg *clabtypes.NodeConfig) error {\n\tm := c.mgmtNetByNetwork(cfg.MgmtNet)\n\tif m.IPAM.Provider == clabtypes.IPAMProviderRuntime {\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\t// tools attach to a network on demand, so it is created even when it is\n\t// flagged as skipped-when-unused by the topology\n\tif err := c.createNetworks(ctx, clabtypes.MgmtNetworks{m}); The mutex protects map access but not the shortname. Hmm — for the API server, deploying two labs concurrently: lab A sets node(network X).shortname = brA, lab B sets = brB, and link creation reads shortname at deploy time → race/wrong bridge. But was this already the case in base? Yes: single shared node with SetMgmtNetUnderlyingBridge(c.Config.Mgmt.Bridge) — two concurrent labs each set the single node. So pre-existing. The per-network map reduces (but doesn't eliminate) cross-lab interference. Not introduced by this PR; not worth flagging maybe as P4 note? Probably not; out of scope.

16. `verifyRootNetNSLinks` change: `append([]clablinks.Node{...}, GetMgmtBrLinkNodes()...)` — append to a 1-element slice literal; GetMgmtBrLinkNodes returns fresh slice, fine.

Wait, but there's a subtle issue: `GetMgmtBrLinkNodes()` *creates* the default node even when no mgmt-net link exists in the topology. In `verifyRootNetNSLinks` (called during config validation), creating the node calls `ns.GetCurrentNS()`. Pre-existing behavior in base? In base, verifyRootNetNSLinks used GetMgmtBrLinkNode() which also created. So same.

17. Now the bigger question — in `deleteNodes`, `GetMgmtBrLinkNodes()` creates the default node if it doesn't exist. In base, `getSpecialLinkNodes()` — let me check what it returns; the diff removed its use in deleteNodes but the function still exists (used elsewhere? line 897 in clab.go). If getSpecialLinkNodes also included "host", the new code preserves it. Let me check.

Let me now read core/clab.go relevant parts, core/apply.go, core/deploy.go, core/topology_reconcile.go around changed areas, runtime docker CreateNet/bridgeNameFromInspect, and links/link.go IsNodeless.
Now let me trace the callers and key helpers.

868: // GetLinkNodes returns all CLab.Nodes nodes as links.Nodes enriched with the special nodes -
869: // host and mgmt-net. The CLab nodes are copied to a new map and thus clab.Node interface is
870: // converted to link.Node.
871: func (c *CLab) getLinkNodes() map[string]clablinks.Node {
872: 	// resolveNodes is a map of all nodes in the topology
873: 	// that is artificially created to combat circular dependencies.
874: 	// If no circ deps were in place we could've used c.Nodes map instead.
875: 	// The map is used to resolve links between the nodes by passing it in the ResolveParams struct.
876: 	resolveNodes := make(map[string]clablinks.Node, len(c.Nodes))
877: 	for k, v := range c.Nodes {
878: 		resolveNodes[k] = v
879: 	}
880: 
881: 	// add the virtual host and mgmt-bridge nodes to the resolve nodes
882: 	specialNodes := c.getSpecialLinkNodes()
883: 	for _, n := range specialNodes {
884: 		resolveNodes[n.GetShortName()] = n
885: 	}
886: 
887: 	return resolveNodes
888: }
889: 
890: // GetSpecialLinkNodes returns a map of special nodes that are used to resolve links.
891: // Special nodes are host and mgmt-bridge nodes that are not typically present in the topology file
892: // but are required to resolve links.
893: func (*CLab) getSpecialLinkNodes() map[string]clablinks.Node {
894: 	// add the virtual host and mgmt-bridge nodes to the resolve nodes
895: 	specialNodes := map[string]clablinks.Node{
896: 		"host":     clablinks.GetHostLinkNode(),
897: 		"mgmt-net": clablinks.GetMgmtBrLinkNode(),
898: 	}
899: 
900: 	return specialNodes
901: }
902: 
903: // ResolveLinks resolves raw links to the actual link types and stores them in the CLab.Links map.
904: func (c *CLab) ResolveLinks() error {
905: 	c.resetResolvedLinks()
906: 
907: 	resolveParams := &clablinks.ResolveParams{
908: 		Nodes:              c.getLinkNodes(),
909: 		DefaultMgmtNetwork: c.Config.Mgmt.Network,
910: 		NodesFilter:        c.nodeFilter,
911: 		LabName:            c.Config.Name,
912: 	}
913: 
914: 	for i, l := range c.Config.Topology.Links {
915: 		l, err := l.Link.Resolve(resolveParams)
916: 		if err != nil {
917: 			return err
918: 		}
919: 
920: 		// if the link is nil, it means that it was filtered out
921: 		if l == nil {
922: 			continue
923: 		}
924: 
925: 		c.Endpoints = append(c.Endpoints, l.GetEndpoints()...)
926: 		c.Links[i] = l
927: 	}
928: 
929: 	return nil
930: }
931: 
932: func (c *CLab) resetResolvedLinks() {
933: 	for _, ep := range c.Endpoints {
934: 		if ep == nil || ep.GetNode() == nil {
935: 			continue
936: 		}
937: 		_ = ep.GetNode().ReleaseEndpoint(ep)
938: 	}
939: 
940: 	c.Endpoints = nil
941: 	c.Links = make(map[int]clablinks.Link)
942: }
943: 
944: // extractDNSServers extracts DNS servers from the resolv.conf files
945: // and populates the Nodes DNS Config with these if not specifically provided.
946: func (c *CLab) extractDNSServers(filesys fs.FS) error {

Reasoning
Important finding candidate: `getSpecialLinkNodes()` still only includes the **default** mgmt-net node (`GetMgmtBrLinkNode()`), keyed as `"mgmt-net"`. But `getLinkNodes()` puts special nodes into `resolveNodes` keyed by `n.GetShortName()`. All `mgmtBridgeLinkNode` instances now share shortname `"mgmt-net"`, so even if all were added, the map would collapse them. But since Resolve uses `getMgmtBrLinkNode(network)` directly (not via the Nodes map), the resolution of mgmt-net links does not go through the map. The map's `mgmt-net` entry is used for what? Probably for endpoint lookup elsewhere or `verifyRootNetNSLinks`. Hmm.

But wait — a real problem: `getSpecialLinkNodes()["mgmt-net"]` returns only the default node, whose shortname is set to the default bridge by setMgmtNetLinkBridges. In `topology_reconcile.go` line 649: `node, exists := c.getSpecialLinkNodes()[nodeName]` — what's this used for? Let me check. If an apply plan needs to look up special nodes for endpoints of non-default mgmt-net links, the endpoint's node is the per-network mgmtBridgeLinkNode, but getSpecialLinkNodes only has the default. Let me check that code.

Also `deploy.go` line 247: `eps := c.getSpecialLinkNodes()["host"].GetEndpoints()` — host only.

Let me look at topology_reconcile.go around 649 and the apply plan endpoints handling.

845: func (c *CLab) reconcileNodes(ctx context.Context, plan *applyPlan) error {
846: 	if plan == nil || plan.currentNodes == nil {
847: 		return nil
848: 	}
849: 
850: 	for nodeName := range plan.currentNodes {
851: 		node, exists := c.Nodes[nodeName]
852: 		if !exists {
853: 			continue
854: 		}
855: 		if _, added := plan.addedNodeSet[nodeName]; added {
856: 			continue
857: 		}
858: 		if plan.isRootNamespaceNode(nodeName) {
859: 			continue
860: 		}
861: 		// Recreated nodes are handled exclusively by the apply pipeline
862: 		// (park -> delete -> deploy -> restore). Calling Reconcile here would
863: 		// delete the container before parkRecreatedNodes can capture its
864: 		// interfaces, breaking the recreate flow.
865: 		if _, recreated := plan.recreatedNodeSet[nodeName]; recreated {
866: 			continue
867: 		}
868: 
869: 		diff := plan.nodeDiffs[nodeName]
870: 
871: 		result, err := node.Reconcile(ctx, diff)
872: 		if err != nil {
873: 			return fmt.Errorf("reconcile failed for node %q: %w", nodeName, err)
874: 		}
875: 
876: 		if result.Action == clabtypes.TopologyDiffActionRestart {
877: 			if err := c.waitNodeRunning(ctx, node); err != nil {
878: 				return err
879: 			}
880: 			if err := c.waitNodeHealthyIfAvailable(ctx, node); err != nil {
881: 				return err
882: 			}
883: 		}
884: 	}
885: 
886: 	return nil
887: }

889: func (p *applyPlan) linkNeedsDeploy(link clablinks.Link) bool {
890: 	for _, ep := range clablinks.RuntimeEndpoints(link) {
891: 		key := endpointKeyFromEndpoint(ep)
892: 		if _, parked := p.parkedNodeSet[key.node]; !parked {
893: 			if _, added := p.addedNodeSet[key.node]; added {
894: 				return true
895: 			}
896: 		}
897: 		if _, live := p.liveEndpointSet[key]; !live {
898: 			return true
899: 		}
900: 	}
901: 
902: 	return !p.linkIntact(link)
903: }
904: 
905: func (p *applyPlan) linkIntact(link clablinks.Link) bool {
906: 	endpoints := clablinks.RuntimeEndpoints(link)
907: 	if len(endpoints) != 2 || len(p.liveEndpointInfo) == 0 {
908: 		return true
909: 	}
910: 
911: 	left, leftOK := p.liveEndpointInfo[endpointKeyFromEndpoint(endpoints[0])]
912: 	right, rightOK := p.liveEndpointInfo[endpointKeyFromEndpoint(endpoints[1])]
913: 	if !leftOK || !rightOK {
914: 		return false
915: 	}
916: 
917: 	if bridgeMasterMismatch(endpoints[0], left) || bridgeMasterMismatch(endpoints[1], right) {
918: 		return false
919: 	}
920: 
921: 	return left.PeerIndex != 0 &&
922: 		right.PeerIndex != 0 &&
923: 		left.PeerIndex == right.Index &&
924: 		right.PeerIndex == left.Index
925: }
926: 
927: // bridgeMasterMismatch reports whether the live interface of a bridge endpoint is
928: // attached to a bridge other than the one the endpoint resolves to, e.g. after a
929: // mgmt-net link moved to another management network. mgmt-net endpoints whose
930: // management network bridge is not resolved are not checked.
931: func bridgeMasterMismatch(ep clablinks.Endpoint, live clablinks.OwnedInterface) bool {
932: 	node := ep.GetNode()
933: 	if node.GetLinkEndpointType() != clablinks.LinkEndpointTypeBridge {
934: 		return false
935: 	}
936: 
937: 	bridge := node.GetShortName()
938: 	if ep.IsNodeless() && (bridge == "" || bridge == "mgmt-net") {
939: 		return false
940: 	}
941: 
942: 	return live.MasterName != bridge
943: }

Reasoning
Now I want to check `endpointKeyFromEndpoint` — how it derives the node name for an endpoint. If it uses `GetShortName()`, then for the mgmt bridge node (shortname = bridge name like "br-lab"), keys would vary by bridge... but the test in apply_test.go asserts `key.node == "mgmt-net"`. So `endpointKeyFromEndpoint` must use the ownership name or special-case mgmt-net. Let me check.

24: func (e *EndpointBridge) Verify(ctx context.Context, p *VerifyLinkParams) error {
25: 	var errs []error
26: 	err := CheckEndpointUniqueness(e)
27: 	if err != nil {
28: 		errs = append(errs, err)
29: 	}
30: 	if (p == nil || p.RunBridgeExistsCheck) &&
31: 		e.Node.GetLinkEndpointType() != LinkEndpointTypeBridgeNS {
32: 		err = CheckBridgeExists(ctx, e.GetNode())

38: 		e.Node.GetLinkEndpointType() != LinkEndpointTypeBridgeNS {
39: 		err = CheckEndpointDoesNotExistYet(ctx, e)

41: 			errs = append(errs, err)

55: 	// the mgmt bridge is nodeless.

60: // CheckBridgeExists verifies that the given bridge is present in the
61: // network namespace referenced via the provided nspath handle.
62: func CheckBridgeExists(ctx context.Context, n Node) error {
63: 	return n.ExecFunction(ctx, func(_ ns.NetNS) error {
64: 		br, err := netlink.LinkByName(n.GetShortName())
65: 		_, notfound := err.(netlink.LinkNotFoundError)
66: 		switch {
67: 		case notfound:
68: 			return fmt.Errorf(
69: 				"bridge %q referenced in topology but does not exist",
70: 				n.GetShortName(),
71: 			)
72: 		case err != nil:
73: 			return err
74: 		case br.Type() != "bridge" && br.Type() != "openvswitch":
75: 			return fmt.Errorf(
76: 				"interface %s found. expected type \"bridge\" or \"openvswitch\", actual is %q",
77: 				n.GetShortName(),
78: 				br.Type(),

Reasoning
`endpointKeyFromEndpoint` uses `ep.IsNodeless() && LinkEndpointTypeBridge` → "mgmt-net". So all mgmt-net endpoints across networks key to node "mgmt-net". Good — that matches the test.

`bridgeMasterMismatch`: for a mgmt-net endpoint with resolved bridge, `bridge := node.GetShortName()` = bridge name (e.g. "br-lab"). `ep.IsNodeless()` = true, but bridge is non-empty and != "mgmt-net", so it does NOT skip — it compares `live.MasterName != bridge`. Good: detects moved link. But note for the default mgmt-net endpoint when c.Config.Mgmt.Bridge is "" (macvlan, or bridge not resolved): bridge == "" → skipped. OK.

Hmm, wait: what about a non-nodeless bridge endpoint (a regular bridge node in topology, LinkEndpointTypeBridge but not nodeless)? `bridge := node.GetShortName()` = the bridge node's name; IsNodeless false → the condition `ep.IsNodeless() && ...` false → compares MasterName != bridge. Same as before. OK, behavior preserved.

Now check `mgmtNetLinkNetworks()` — it silently ignores errors from `mgmtNetByName` (`if m, err := ...; err == nil`). But `validateManagementLinks` (called during initMgmtNetwork) errors on unknown networks. However `mgmtNetLinkNetworks` is also called from `skipMgmtNetwork` and `setMgmtNetLinkBridges` — in contexts where validation has run. Except: destroy with WithSkippedMgmtNetLinksCheck — validation skipped, so a topology referencing an undefined network: `mgmtNetByName` errors, silently dropped from mgmtNetLinkNetworks. Then in ResolveLinks, `LinkMgmtNetRaw.Resolve` is called with network X: getMgmtBrLinkNode(X) creates a node keyed "X" — fine, no error. Then setMgmtNetLinkBridges doesn't set a bridge for it (not in mgmtNetLinkNetworks), so node shortname stays "mgmt-net". Destroy doesn't deploy links, fine.

Wait, but there's the test TestMakeCopyForDestroyIgnoresMgmtNetLinkChecks which expects makeCopyForDestroy to succeed. OK.

Now — the interesting scenario: **apply on a running lab where a mgmt-net link is added selecting a non-default network that doesn't exist yet**. Flow: apply() → setMgmtBridgeFromRuntime → resolveMgmtNetLinkNetworks (network doesn't exist → Bridge stays "") → setMgmtNetLinkBridges → SetMgmtNetworkUnderlyingBridge(X, "") → node shortname = "". Then ResolveLinks → link resolves with the node. Then checkApplyTopologyDefinition → verifyLinks → CheckBridgeExists for the bridge endpoint? Let me check verifyLinks params: params.RunBridgeExistsCheck — in checkApplyTopologyDefinition, params = *clablinks.NewVerifyLinkParams() with AllowExistingEndpoint = true. Does NewVerifyLinkParams set RunBridgeExistsCheck? Let me check. If yes, CheckBridgeExists(ctx, node) → netlink.LinkByName("") → LinkNotFoundError → error "bridge \"\" referenced in topology but does not exist". That's a confusing error message for a missing network that apply is about to create. Hmm wait, but does apply create missing management networks? Let's check prepareApply → prepareLabManagementNetwork → creates networks? Let me read prepareLabManagementNetwork in deploy.go.

Actually wait — is this scenario reachable? apply on existing lab: nodes exist. User edits topology to add a mgmt-net link with `network: newnet` where newnet is defined in mgmt with skip-when-unused... or even without skip-when-unused, the network isn't created unless deploy creates it. Does apply create missing management networks? prepareLabManagementNetwork presumably creates missing mgmt networks (allocate IPs etc.). Let me read deploy.go prepareLabManagementNetwork fully.

30: // DeployResult holds the outcome of a Deploy call.
31: type DeployResult struct {
32: 	Containers []clabruntime.GenericContainer
33: 	// Apply summarizes the reconciliation of an already deployed lab or the dry-run plan;
34: 	// it is nil when Deploy performed a fresh full deployment.
35: 	Apply *ApplyResult
36: 	// RuntimePlan is the remote resource plan produced by a controller-driven runtime dry-run.
37: 	RuntimePlan *clablabruntime.DeployPlan
38: }
39: 
40: // Deploy converges the lab to the requested topology. A lab without runtime state is
41: // deployed from scratch, an already deployed lab is reconciled in place.
42: func (c *CLab) Deploy(
43: 	ctx context.Context,
44: 	options *DeployOptions,
45: ) (*DeployResult, error) {
46: 	if options == nil {
47: 		var err error
48: 		options, err = NewDeployOptions(0)
49: 		if err != nil {
50: 			return nil, err
51: 		}
52: 	}
53: 
54: 	if options.reconfigure {
55: 		if options.dryRun {
56: 			return nil, fmt.Errorf(
57: 				"dry-run cannot be combined with reconfigure: " +
58: 					"reconfigure always destroys and redeploys the full lab",
59: 			)
60: 		}
61: 
62: 		containers, err := c.deploy(ctx, options)
63: 		if err != nil {
64: 			return nil, err
65: 		}
66: 
67: 		return &DeployResult{Containers: containers}, nil
68: 	}
69: 
70: 	if c.LabRuntime != nil && options.dryRun {
71: 		planner, ok := c.LabRuntime.(clablabruntime.TopologyPlanner)
72: 		if !ok {
73: 			return nil, fmt.Errorf("lab runtime %q does not support dry-run", c.globalRuntimeName)
74: 		}

147: // checkReconcileDeployOptions rejects deploy options that only make sense for a fresh
148: // deployment when deploy is about to reconcile an already deployed lab.
149: func (c *CLab) checkReconcileDeployOptions(options *DeployOptions) error {
150: 	if c.managementNetworkOverridden {
151: 		return fmt.Errorf(
152: 			"management network overrides require a fresh deployment, but lab %q is already "+

171: 			c.Config.Name,
172: 		)
173: 	}

Reasoning
Good — `bridgeNameFromInspect` is a pre-existing helper reused in `ResolveMgmtBridge`. Reuse is good.

Now check deploy flow: fresh deploy: `ResolveLinks()` is called BEFORE `prepareLabManagementNetwork`! Line 190: `err = c.ResolveLinks()` then line 206 `c.prepareLabManagementNetwork(ctx)` (which creates networks then calls `setMgmtNetLinkBridges`). So during fresh deploy: ResolveLinks resolves mgmt-net links with the mgmt nodes whose shortnames are still stale/"" — but resolution just creates endpoint objects; the bridge shortname is read later at deploy time (`AddLinkToContainer` uses `b.shortname` → `netlink.LinkByName`). So order is fine: setMgmtNetLinkBridges after CreateNetwork sets the bridge names before endpoints are deployed (createNodes comes after prepareLabManagementNetwork at line 224). Good.

But wait: `setMgmtNetLinkBridges` iterates `mgmtNetLinkNetworks()` — for networks where `m.Bridge == ""` (e.g., network exists check failed / skipMgmtNetwork skipped a network?) — hmm. Consider: a mgmt-net link selects network X with skip-when-unused: true, and no nodes attach to X (only the mgmt-net link). skipMgmtNetwork now keeps X because mgmtNetLinkNetworks contains X. Good — covered by the test.

Now consider: network X used by mgmt-net link, X's driver is bridge but `m.Bridge` is "" after CreateNet? Does docker CreateNet set m.Bridge? Let me check CreateNet around line 372-420.

663: // bridgeNameFromInspect resolves the underlying linux bridge name from a docker network inspect
664: // response.
665: func bridgeNameFromInspect(netResource *networkapi.Inspect, mgmtNetwork string) (string, error) {

So CreateNet sets d.mgmt.Bridge for bridged networks. And `mgmtRuntime` binds runtime to the network via ForMgmtNet(m) sharing the same *MgmtNet pointer — so m.Bridge gets set during CreateNetwork. Good.

Now, potential issue: podman's ResolveMgmtBridge does `ctx, err := r.connect(ctx)` — check that podman's other methods use `r.connect(ctx)` similarly; and whether `r.mgmt` is the bound mgmt. Let me check podman.go ForMgmtNet / connect.

Next, key concern #1: In `apply` (reconcile path) with a **newly added mgmt-net link to a network that doesn't exist yet** — does apply create the missing network? apply path: prepareApply → prepareLabManagementNetwork → CreateNetwork → creates networks (skipMgmtNetwork now keeps the network since mgmt-net link uses it). So during apply, the network is created in prepareApply, which happens AFTER setMgmtNetLinkBridges at line 138! Wait: apply order:

1. setMgmtBridgeFromRuntime(currentNodes) — sets c.Config.Mgmt.Bridge from runtime.
2. resolveMgmtNetLinkNetworks(ctx) — looks up bridges for non-default networks; missing → unresolved (Bridge = "").
3. setMgmtNetLinkBridges() — sets node shortname = m.Bridge (possibly "" for missing networks).
4. ResolveLinks() — links resolved.
5. checkApplyTopologyDefinition(ctx) — verifyLinks: CheckBridgeExists for bridge endpoints? Let's check: params := *clablinks.NewVerifyLinkParams(); does it have RunBridgeExistsCheck true? If so, CheckBridgeExists(ctx, node) with shortname "" → netlink.LinkByName("") → LinkNotFoundError → error "bridge \"\" referenced in topology but does not exist". Hmm, but wait — for mgmt-net endpoints, is the Verify check run? EndpointBridge.Verify runs CheckBridgeExists unless RunBridgeExistsCheck is false or node type is BridgeNS. Let me check NewVerifyLinkParams.

Also note in step 5, this would fail for the *unresolved* new network. But does apply ever create the network before step 5? No — prepareApply runs later. So adding a mgmt-net link selecting a brand-new network to an existing lab would fail at verification with "bridge \"\" referenced in topology but does not exist" — a confusing error. Unless verifyLinks skips mgmt-net endpoints. Let me check CheckEndpointUniqueness / how verifyLinks treats mgmt-net links, and NewVerifyLinkParams.

Actually wait — also `CheckEndpointDoesNotExistYet` skipped when AllowExistingEndpoint. Let me read verifyLinks and NewVerifyLinkParams.

Also concern #2: `setMgmtBridgeFromRuntime` — what does it set? Only c.Config.Mgmt.Bridge for the default? Let me check it.

Reasoning
So `RunBridgeExistsCheck: true` by default. In apply's `checkApplyTopologyDefinition`, params.RunBridgeExistsCheck stays true (only AllowExistingEndpoint set to true). So `verifyLinks` → EndpointBridge.Verify → CheckBridgeExists(ctx, node) where node is the mgmtBridgeLinkNode with shortname = bridge name.

Scenario: existing lab, user adds a mgmt-net link selecting a **new** non-default management network (defined in mgmt but not yet created). Flow in apply:
1. `resolveMgmtNetLinkNetworks`: the network doesn't exist → Bridge stays "".
2. `setMgmtNetLinkBridges`: node shortname = "" for that network.
3. `ResolveLinks`.
4. `checkApplyTopologyDefinition` → `verifyLinks` → for the new mgmt-net endpoint: CheckBridgeExists → `netlink.LinkByName("")` → LinkNotFoundError → error: `bridge "" referenced in topology but does not exist`.

So applying a topology that adds a mgmt-net link to a not-yet-created non-default network fails with a cryptic error, and the network is never created (CreateNetwork runs later in prepareApply). Wait — but actually is this scenario realistic? A user adding `mgmt: - network: lab` and `links: - type: mgmt-net, network: lab` to an existing lab, then running `clab deploy` (reconcile path) or `clab apply`. Deploy reconcile path → apply. Yes, reachable.

Hold on — is the network really not created? CreateNetwork is in prepareLabManagementNetwork called in prepareApply — after the verification failure. So apply aborts. The user sees `bridge "" referenced in topology but does not exist`. Confusing, but it's an error, not silent misbehavior. However — hmm, wait. Was this scenario reachable in base? In base, mgmt-net links could only attach to the default network, whose bridge exists (deployed lab). So this is a new failure mode introduced by the PR. Severity: P2 or P3? It blocks a legitimate operation (adding a mgmt-net link on a new network via apply) with a cryptic error. Actually wait — let me double-check: would the mgmt network be created on a fresh `deploy` after? The lab is already deployed; `clab deploy` reconciles → apply → fails. Only `--reconfigure` or destroy+deploy works. Hmm.

But wait, actually let me double check verifyLinks flow for mgmt-net endpoints: does verifyLinks skip nodeless endpoints? Let me look at verifyLinks implementation and how it filters endpoints.

Reasoning
Confirmed: in apply's `checkApplyTopologyDefinition` (core/apply.go:273), `verifyLinks` runs with `RunBridgeExistsCheck: true`, and the new mgmt-net endpoint for an unresolved network has `GetShortName() == ""` → `CheckBridgeExists` → `netlink.LinkByName("")` → LinkNotFoundError → `bridge "" referenced in topology but does not exist`.

But wait — is there a guard for the empty bridge? In EndpointBridge.Verify: `if (p == nil || p.RunBridgeExistsCheck) && e.Node.GetLinkEndpointType() != LinkEndpointTypeBridgeNS` — mgmtBridgeLinkNode type is LinkEndpointTypeBridge, not BridgeNS. So yes, CheckBridgeExists runs.

Hmm, but wait — in the fresh-deploy path, `checkTopologyDefinition` runs AFTER prepareLabManagementNetwork, so bridges are set. In apply path, the check runs BEFORE prepareApply (which creates networks). So adding a mgmt-net link to a new non-default network via apply fails before network creation.

But hold on — is that really the case in base for the default network? In base (and in head for the default network), apply on existing lab: c.Config.Mgmt.Bridge is resolved from runtime by setMgmtBridgeFromRuntime. So the default network always resolves. For a new *non-default* network, there's no bridge until created. So yes, this is a new failure mode.

Also what about the fresh-deploy path via apply? initialDeploy → c.deploy(...) → deploy() → ResolveLinks() → prepareLabManagementNetwork (creates networks, sets bridges) → checkTopologyDefinition. Fine.

What about podman? Podman's MgmtBridgeResolver: does PodmanRuntime support ForMgmtNet (MgmtNetBinder)? Check podman.go for ForMgmtNet. If podman doesn't implement MgmtNetBinder, mgmtRuntime returns error for non-default network → resolveMgmtNetLinkNetworks logs debug and skips → mgmt-net link to non-default network with podman resolves to shortname ""... wait no. setMgmtNetLinkBridges would call SetMgmtNetworkUnderlyingBridge(m.Network, m.Bridge) where m.Bridge = "" → shortname "". Then fresh deploy: CreateNetwork → createNetworks → mgmtRuntime(c.globalRuntimeName, m) errors → CreateNetwork fails with "runtime X does not support multiple management networks". OK so podman fails cleanly on fresh deploy. Let me check which runtimes implement MgmtNetBinder.

17: func (c *CLab) CreateNetwork(ctx context.Context) error {
18: 	defined := c.allMgmtNetworks()
19: 	networks := make(clabtypes.MgmtNetworks, 0, len(defined))
20: 	for _, m := range defined {
21: 		if c.skipMgmtNetwork(m) {
22: 			log.Debug("Skipping management network, no node uses it", "network", m.Network)
23: 			continue
24: 		}
25: 		networks = append(networks, m)
26: 	}
27: 	return c.createNetworks(ctx, networks)
28: }

30: // createNetworks creates or inspects the given management networks
31: // and labels the nodes with their management bridge.
32: func (c *CLab) createNetworks(ctx context.Context, networks clabtypes.MgmtNetworks) error {
33: 	for _, m := range networks {
34: 		var opts []clabruntime.NetworkCreateOptions
35: 		if addresses := c.staticManagementAddresses(m); len(addresses) != 0 {
36: 			opts = append(opts, clabruntime.NetworkCreateOptions{StaticAddresses: addresses})
37: 		}
38: 
39: 		rt, err := c.mgmtRuntime(c.globalRuntimeName, m)
40: 		if err != nil {
41: 			return err
42: 		}
43: 		// create docker network or use existing one
44: 		if err := rt.CreateNet(ctx, opts...); err != nil {
45: 			return err
46: 		}
47: 
48: 		// VM kinds read the subnets at init, before the runtime resolved them
49: 		for _, cfg := range c.mgmtNetNodes(m) {
50: 			for key, subnet := range map[string]string{
51: 				"DOCKER_NET_V4_ADDR": m.IPv4Subnet,
52: 				"DOCKER_NET_V6_ADDR": m.IPv6Subnet,
53: 			} {
54: 				if value, ok := cfg.Env[key]; ok && value == "" {
55: 					cfg.Env[key] = subnet
56: 				}
57: 			}
58: 		}
59: 	}
60: 
61: 	// save mgmt bridge name as a label
62: 	for _, n := range c.Nodes {
63: 		bridge := c.globalRuntime().Mgmt().Bridge
64: 		if m := c.mgmtNetByNetwork(n.Config().MgmtNet); m != c.Config.Mgmt {
65: 			bridge = m.Bridge
66: 		}
67: 		n.Config().Labels[clabconstants.NodeMgmtNetBr] = bridge
68: 	}
69: 
70: 	return nil
71: }
72: 
73: // mgmtNetNodes returns the configs of the nodes attached to the given management network.
74: func (c *CLab) mgmtNetNodes(m *clabtypes.MgmtNet) []*clabtypes.NodeConfig {
75: 	var configs []*clabtypes.NodeConfig
76: 	for _, node := range c.Nodes {
77: 		if c.mgmtNetByNetwork(node.Config().MgmtNet) == m {
78: 			configs = append(configs, node.Config())
79: 		}

81: 	return configs
82: }

84: // SyncMgmtHostRoutes reconciles host routes to current macvlan management endpoints.
85: func (c *CLab) SyncMgmtHostRoutes(ctx context.Context) error {
86: 	if c.Config == nil || c.Config.Mgmt == nil {
87: 		return nil
88: 	}

Reasoning
Key observations:

1. `setMgmtNetLinkBridges` (core/network.go:207-220): for non-default networks with `m.Bridge == ""` (network doesn't exist yet during apply, or resolution skipped), it calls `clablinks.SetMgmtNetworkUnderlyingBridge(m.Network, "")` — setting the node shortname to `""`. Then in apply, `verifyLinks` → `EndpointBridge.Verify` with `RunBridgeExistsCheck: true` → `CheckBridgeExists` → `netlink.LinkByName("")` → error `bridge "" referenced in topology but does not exist`. So adding a mgmt-net link on a brand-new non-default network to an already-deployed lab (reconcile path via `deploy` or `clab apply`) fails before `prepareApply` creates the network. Also in `bridgeMasterMismatch`: `bridge == ""` → skipped, so the mismatch check silently passes — fine.

Wait, actually — need to double-check whether `EndpointBridge.Verify` runs for the mgmt-net endpoint. The mgmt bridge node is of type `LinkEndpointTypeBridge` (not BridgeNS)? Let me check link_mgmt-net.go for the node type. Also whether the mgmt-net endpoint verify is special-cased. Let me read link_mgmt-net.go fully.

Also need to double check apply order — read apply.go around lines 100-160 and 260-290.

2. `mgmtRuntime` error: read network.go earlier? `c.mgmtRuntime(c.globalRuntimeName, m)` — need to see its implementation to know the error for podman. It's in clab.go probably. For podman (no ForMgmtNet), CreateNetwork → createNetworks → mgmtRuntime error → fresh deploy fails with "does not support multiple management networks". That's a clean error, acceptable (documented?). Check docs.

3. `createNetworks` label stamping (lines 62-68): `bridge := c.globalRuntime().Mgmt().Bridge; if m := c.mgmtNetByNetwork(n.Config().MgmtNet); m != c.Config.Mgmt { bridge = m.Bridge }`. Wait — bug? For a node whose MgmtNet is empty (default), `mgmtNetByNetwork("")` presumably returns c.Config.Mgmt → bridge = default bridge. For a node on network X → bridge = X.Bridge. OK. But what about nodes on the default network — `c.globalRuntime().Mgmt().Bridge`: is the global runtime's mgmt the same pointer as c.Config.Mgmt? Typically yes (ForMgmtNet shares config). OK.

But subtle: `mgmtNetByNetwork(n.Config().MgmtNet)` — what does it return for an unknown network? Probably the default. Fine.

4. Podman `ResolveMgmtBridge`: `details.NetworkInterface` — is that the bridge name? For podman bridge networks, NetworkInterface is the bridge interface name. OK.

But note podman's `ResolveMgmtBridge` is only reachable if `mgmtRuntime` succeeds — which requires ForMgmtNet. Podman doesn't implement MgmtNetBinder, so `mgmtRuntime` errors → skipped (log.Debug). So podman's ResolveMgmtBridge is only used for the default network (from `setMgmtBridgeFromRuntime`? no — that uses labels). Where else is MgmtBridgeResolver used? destroy's `resolveExistingMgmtNetworks`. Podman: destroy for non-default networks → mgmtRuntime error → skipped. Hmm wait — then podman can't destroy non-default mgmt networks. But podman also can't create them (CreateNetwork fails). But wait — podman could have created them outside containerlab, or from an older version? Edge case; the destroy path handles "networks that mgmtRuntime can't bind" by skipping. Hmm, but actually — for destroy with bridged networks, `resolveExistingMgmtNetworks(ctx, bridged)` — let me read destroy.go.

Let me now read:
- apply.go (order + checkApplyTopologyDefinition)
- link_mgmt-net.go (node type, getMgmtBrLinkNode)
- destroy.go

4: 	"context"
5: 	"fmt"
6: 	"net/netip"
7: 
8: 	clabtypes "github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/types"
9: 
10: 	clablinks "github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/links"

13: // ApplyResult summarizes the changes applied by an apply operation.
14: type ApplyResult struct {
15: 	DryRun           bool     `json:"dry-run"`
16: 	DeployedLab      bool     `json:"deployed-lab"`
17: 	LabName          string   `json:"lab-name,omitempty"`
18: 	AddedNodes       []string `json:"added-nodes"`
19: 	DeletedNodes     []string `json:"deleted-nodes"`
20: 	RecreatedNodes   []string `json:"recreated-nodes"`
21: 	StartedNodes     []string `json:"started-nodes"`
22: 	AddedLinks       []string `json:"added-links"`
23: 	DeletedEndpoints []string `json:"deleted-endpoints"`
24: 	RestartedNodes   []string `json:"restarted-nodes"`
25: 	// NodeChangeReasons explains per node why apply restarts or recreates it,
26: 	// e.g. "added link" or "config drift: image".
27: 	NodeChangeReasons map[string]string `json:"node-change-reasons,omitempty"`
28: }
29: 
30: func applyResultFromPlan(plan *applyPlan) *ApplyResult {
31: 	result := &ApplyResult{
32: 		AddedNodes:        []string{},
33: 		DeletedNodes:      []string{},
34: 		RecreatedNodes:    []string{},
35: 		StartedNodes:      []string{},
36: 		AddedLinks:        []string{},
37: 		DeletedEndpoints:  []string{},
38: 		RestartedNodes:    []string{},
39: 		NodeChangeReasons: map[string]string{},
40: 	}
41: 	if plan == nil {
42: 		return result
43: 	}
44: 
45: 	result.AddedNodes = sortedStringSet(plan.addedNodeSet)
46: 	result.DeletedNodes = sortedStringSet(plan.deletedNodeSet)
47: 	result.RecreatedNodes = sortedStringSet(plan.recreatedNodeSet)
48: 	result.StartedNodes = sortedStringSet(plan.startNodeSet)
49: 	result.AddedLinks = applyLinkNames(plan.addedLinks)
50: 	result.DeletedEndpoints = applyDeletedEndpointNames(plan.staleEndpoints)
51: 	result.RestartedNodes = sortedStringSet(unionStringSets(
52: 		plan.restartNodeSet,
53: 		plan.linkRestartNodeSet,
54: 	))
55: 
56: 	for nodeName, reason := range plan.nodeChangeReasons {
57: 		result.NodeChangeReasons[nodeName] = reason
58: 	}
59: 
60: 	return result
61: }
62: 
63: // Apply deploys a lab if it is absent, otherwise it aligns the deployed lab with the
64: // desired topology using runtime state as baseline.
65: func (c *CLab) Apply(
66: 	ctx context.Context,
67: 	options *ApplyOptions,
68: ) (*ApplyResult, error) {
69: 	if options == nil {
70: 		var err error
71: 		options, err = NewApplyOptions(0)
72: 		if err != nil {
73: 			return nil, err
74: 		}
75: 	}
76: 
77: 	if c.Config.Name == "" {
78: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("missing lab name")
79: 	}
80: 	if !c.TopoPaths.TopologyFileIsSet() {
81: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf(
82: 			"apply requires a topology file; provide --topo when it cannot be derived from --name",
83: 		)
84: 	}
85: 
86: 	currentNodes, err := c.runtimeNodeGroups(ctx)
87: 	if err != nil {
88: 		return nil, err
89: 	}
90: 
91: 	return c.apply(ctx, options, currentNodes)
92: }
93: 
94: func (c *CLab) apply(
95: 	ctx context.Context,
96: 	options *ApplyOptions,
97: 	currentNodes map[string]*runtimeNodeGroup,
98: ) (*ApplyResult, error) {
99: 	initialDeploy, err := c.needsInitialDeploy(currentNodes)
100: 	if err != nil {
101: 		return nil, err
102: 	}
103: 	if initialDeploy {
104: 		result := &ApplyResult{
105: 			DryRun:      options.dryRun,
106: 			DeployedLab: true,
107: 			LabName:     c.Config.Name,
108: 		}
109: 
110: 		if options.dryRun {
111: 			return result, nil
112: 		}
113: 
114: 		deployOptions, err := NewDeployOptions(options.maxWorkers)
115: 		if err != nil {
116: 			return nil, err
117: 		}
118: 		deployOptions.SetSkipPostDeploy(options.skipPostDeploy).
119: 			SetSkipLabDirFileACLs(options.skipLabDirFileACLs).
120: 			SetGraph(options.graph).
121: 			SetExportTemplate(options.exportTemplate)
122: 
123: 		if _, err := c.deploy(ctx, deployOptions); err != nil {
124: 			return nil, err
125: 		}
126: 
127: 		return result, nil
128: 	}
129: 
130: 	if err := c.setMgmtBridgeFromRuntime(currentNodes); err != nil {
131: 		return nil, err
132: 	}
133: 
134: 	if err := c.resolveMgmtNetLinkNetworks(ctx); err != nil {
135: 		return nil, err
136: 	}
137: 
138: 	if err := c.setMgmtNetLinkBridges(); err != nil {
139: 		return nil, err
140: 	}
141: 
142: 	if err := c.ResolveLinks(); err != nil {
143: 		return nil, err
144: 	}
145: 
146: 	if err := c.checkApplyTopologyDefinition(ctx); err != nil {
147: 		return nil, err
148: 	}
149: 
150: 	plan, err := c.planApply(ctx, currentNodes)
151: 	if err != nil {
152: 		return nil, err
153: 	}
154: 
155: 	if options.dryRun {
156: 		result := applyResultFromPlan(plan)
157: 		result.DryRun = true
158: 		return result, nil
159: 	}
160: 
161: 	if err := c.reconcileNodes(ctx, plan); err != nil {
162: 		return nil, err
163: 	}
164: 
165: 	if plan.empty() {
166: 		if c.tailscaleProxyEnabled() {
167: 			if err := c.updateRuntimeInfoForExistingNodes(ctx); err != nil {
168: 				return nil, err
169: 			}
170: 		}
171: 		if err := c.syncTailscaleProxy(ctx); err != nil {
172: 			return nil, err
173: 		}
174: 		if options.finalizeNoop {
175: 			if err := c.prepareApply(
176: 				ctx,
177: 				nil,
178: 				options.skipLabDirFileACLs,
179: 				currentNodes,
180: 			); err != nil {
181: 				return nil, err
182: 			}
183: 		}
184: 		if err := c.SyncMgmtHostRoutes(ctx); err != nil {
185: 			return nil, err
186: 		}
187: 		if options.finalizeNoop {
188: 			if _, err := c.finalize(ctx, options.exportTemplate, options.graph); err != nil {
189: 				return nil, err
190: 			}
191: 		} else {
192: 			c.writeState()
193: 		}
194: 		return applyResultFromPlan(plan), nil
195: 	}
196: 
197: 	deployNodeNames := plan.deployNodeNames()
198: 	if err := c.prepareApply(
199: 		ctx,
200: 		deployNodeNames,
201: 		options.skipLabDirFileACLs,
202: 		currentNodes,
203: 	); err != nil {
204: 		return nil, err
205: 	}
206: 
207: 	if err := c.deleteApplyEndpoints(ctx, plan.staleEndpoints); err != nil {
208: 		return nil, err
209: 	}
210: 
211: 	if err := c.parkRecreatedNodes(ctx, plan); err != nil {
212: 		return nil, err
213: 	}
214: 
215: 	if err := c.deleteApplyNodes(ctx, plan); err != nil {
216: 		return nil, err
217: 	}
218: 
219: 	if err := c.deployApplyNodes(ctx, plan, options.maxWorkers); err != nil {
220: 		return nil, err
221: 	}
222: 
223: 	if err := c.restoreRecreatedNodes(ctx, plan); err != nil {
224: 		return nil, err
225: 	}
226: 
227: 	if err := c.removeApplyLinkEndpoints(ctx, plan.addedLinks); err != nil {
228: 		return nil, err
229: 	}
230: 
231: 	if err := c.deployLinks(ctx, plan.addedLinks, deployNodeNames); err != nil {
232: 		return nil, err
233: 	}
234: 
235: 	if err := c.postDeployApplyNodes(ctx, deployNodeNames, options.skipPostDeploy); err != nil {
236: 		return nil, err
237: 	}
238: 
239: 	if err := c.restartApplyNodes(ctx, plan.linkRestartNodeSet); err != nil {
240: 		return nil, err
241: 	}
242: 
243: 	if err := c.updateRuntimeInfoForExistingNodes(ctx); err != nil {
244: 		return nil, err
245: 	}
246: 	if err := c.syncTailscaleProxy(ctx); err != nil {
247: 		return nil, err
248: 	}
249: 	if err := c.SyncMgmtHostRoutes(ctx); err != nil {
250: 		return nil, err
251: 	}
252: 
253: 	if _, err := c.finalize(ctx, options.exportTemplate, options.graph); err != nil {
254: 		return nil, err
255: 	}
256: 
257: 	return applyResultFromPlan(plan), nil
258: }
259: 
260: func (c *CLab) checkApplyTopologyDefinition(ctx context.Context) error {
261: 	params := *clablinks.NewVerifyLinkParams()
262: 	if len(c.Endpoints) > 0 {
263: 		runtime := c.globalRuntime()
264: 		if runtime == nil {
265: 			return fmt.Errorf("container runtime is not initialized")
266: 		}
267: 		config := runtime.Config()
268: 		if config.VerifyLinkParams != nil {
269: 			params = *config.VerifyLinkParams
270: 		}
271: 	}
272: 	params.AllowExistingEndpoint = true
273: 	if err := c.verifyLinks(ctx, &params); err != nil {
274: 		return err
275: 	}
276: 
277: 	if err := c.verifyRootNetNSLinks(); err != nil {
278: 		return err
279: 	}
280: 
281: 	for _, nodeName := range sortedNodeNames(c.Nodes) {
282: 		if err := c.Nodes[nodeName].CheckDeploymentConditions(ctx); err != nil {
283: 			return err
284: 		}
285: 	}
286: 
287: 	if err := c.verifyDuplicateAddresses(); err != nil {
288: 		return err
289: 	}
290: 
291: 	return c.verifyTailscaleProxy()
292: }

297: 	skipLabDirFileACLs bool,
